# cyphersolver

An informal benchmark of frontier AI models on historically "unsolved" ciphers, as much a hobby as a measurement.
Most of the work is by Claude (Opus 5 and 5.5, some Fable 5.1); a few results came from outside contributors using
other models. Each target's `profile.json` records which model did what, and what it was given. The first targets
came from three standard lists: S. Tomokiyo's
[Unsolved Historical Ciphers](https://cryptiana.web.fc2.com/code/unsolved.htm), Klaus Schmeh's
[Top 50 unsolved encrypted messages](https://scienceblogs.de/klausis-krypto-kolumne/the-top-50-unsolved-encrypted-messages/),
and Elonka Dunin's [famous unsolved codes](https://elonka.com/UnsolvedCodes.html). Since mid-September 2026 most come
from this project's own catalogue of undeciphered archival letters (DECODE records, BnF and Gallica notices,
Tomokiyo's "undeciphered" letters, printed calendars). Each one is a test of how far an AI assistant, working with a
human, can get on a problem that has resisted people for decades or centuries: how much is archival research, how much
is cryptanalysis, and where it stops. Method: archival research, historical cribs, printed editions, and small
purpose-built solvers, always run against matched controls so that a negative result says something. The results
are being written up with George Lasry as a paper on how LLMs perform against historical ciphers.

- **Contributing:** the ledger takes contributions from anyone: a solution to a listed cipher, a new unsolved cipher,
  a new cipher with its solution, or an independent check of a reading. See [CONTRIBUTING.md](CONTRIBUTING.md) and the
  site's [How to contribute](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/contribute.html); everyone credited is on
  [Contributors](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/contributors.html), built from `outcome.credits` in each
  `profile.json` and the registry [`contributors.json`](contributors.json).
- **Website:** https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/ — hub, priority queue, and formal write-ups (source in [`docs/`](docs/)).
- **Solved, catalogued:** [SOLVED_CATALOGUE.md](SOLVED_CATALOGUE.md) — every item solved, read or partly read, the method that broke it, and where the AI did the work.
- **Solved, ranked:** [SOLVED_RANKING.md](SOLVED_RANKING.md) — the finished targets in one table, scored by difficulty, historical weight, novelty, research effort, profile and verification.
- **To the archive:** [site page](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/archives.html) · [`archive_requests.json`](archive_requests.json) — the gaps that need a reading room, a reproduction order or a library, by archive, for volunteers to take on.
- **Catalogue of open targets:** [CATALOGUE.md](CATALOGUE.md) · [site page](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/catalogue.html) — undeciphered letters and series absent from the standard lists, scored for importance, solvability and difficulty. Generated from [`catalogue.json`](catalogue.json); a target leaves it when it is read, resolved or found in print. How it was compiled and refilled (BnF/Gallica, DECODE, the 22 Sept 2026 new-solves sweep) is in CATALOGUE.md; harvest scripts in [`research/catalogue_harvest/`](research/catalogue_harvest/).
- **Per-target record:** each folder under [`targets/`](targets/) has a `NOTES.md` (sources, dead ends, what is established and what is inferred) and a `profile.json` (the fixed-field record behind the paper; [`profile.schema.json`](profile.schema.json)).

|---|---|---|

| [Key recovered based on adjacent plaintext](#key-recovered-based-on-adjacent-plaintext) | 88 | 38 |

| [Read with known key](#read-with-known-key) | 103 | 51 |

| [In progress](#in-progress) | 11 | &mdash; |

## Results

The third column of each table is the day the finding landed in this repository: the date of the first commit
that carried it, taken from the git history, not the date of the document. The write-up pages carry the same
date, and `docs/_dates.json` holds them all. "Date" is the date of the document itself.

### Key recovered from ciphertext-only

Key rebuilt here from the ciphertext alone. Independent re-solutions of items solved elsewhere are marked.

| Target | Date | Landed | Extent | Result | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Afonso VI (countersigned Castelo Melhor) → Sancho Manuel de Vilhena, Count of Vila Flor**, general of the Alentejo army, Lisbon; ANTT Luís de Miranda Pereira liv. 2 (PT/TT/LMP/0002), images 87; 53–57 and 59; 69 and 71; 37 and 39 (DigitArq); catalogue 391 (dated there 1660–61) | 13–26 May 1663 | 27 Oct 2026 | complete | **Key recovered from ciphertext-only by Brice Dudout** (with Claude Opus 5.5; [his write-up](https://bdudout.github.io/europe-chiffree/en/documents.html#vilaflor-1663); contributed): letter substitution with homophones on Portuguese (numbers 2–53 and letter-like symbols), three states of the key (K1–K3). K1 recovered from the 26 May letter alone, frozen, and tested on the 13 May letter transcribed without it (−2.61 per character, controls at best −4.52); second copies enciphered with other homophones and a sentence clear in copy 1 and in cipher in copy 2 (164 of 166 characters) confirm it; validation pass. Four letters: 13 May (the cavalry on its way), 15 May long letter (reinforcements, pay, the King to Setúbal) and note, 26 May ("ultima rezolução"): relieve Évora "a todo o risco", even at the cost of a battle. 1,952 of 1,953 transcribed symbols carry a value; about fifteen slips in 13 May corrected from the sense; ʒ and "Lo[bo] ‹aal› de Agalega" open. Leaves m0057/m0059 swapped in the binding. Of the long 15 May letter only a 174-symbol passage is transcribed in the repository. | [`targets/vilaflor1663/`](targets/vilaflor1663/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/vilaflor1663.html) |
| **Lope Hurtado de Mendoza → Charles V**, Tordesillas, AGS Patronato Real, PTR,LEG,2,36,8, ff. 1r–1v (PARES 12988891); catalogue 626 (class B) | 10 Dec 1520 | 27 Oct 2026 | complete | **Key recovered from ciphertext-only by Brice Dudout** (with Claude Opus 5.5; [his edition](https://bdudout.github.io/europe-chiffree/en/documents.html#lope-hurtado-1520), PR #138; contributed): a letter wholly in cipher, the confidential companion of the clear letter of the same day (36,6, printed by Danvila, MHE XXXVI pp. 688–690). The key J. Paz's slip points to, in the letter of 23 Sept 1520 (36,2), does not fit. Homophonic letter substitution of about 37 signs, words kept apart, some signs with two or three values, dotted sigla for persons, the last lines mixed with clear words; K1 built on recto ll. 1–16 and frozen, held-out verso ll. 11–17 give 0.827 of words in a Spanish lexicon (200 shuffled keys: median 0.288, max 0.596); validation pass (−2.139 per letter against −3.118 for the best shuffled refit). About 97.7% of some 920 cipher words give sense (95.4% certain): the ·fre·, probably the Admiral, wants a settlement with the Junta, the Marquis of Denia is hated, the army is "muy mal mandada", the see of Jaén and every vacancy to be held back. Twelve doubtful signs settled by Dudout's own look at the images. Open: about 21 words, one sign not in the table, the sigla other than ·fre· | [`targets/lopehurtado1520/`](targets/lopehurtado1520/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/lopehurtado1520.html) |
| **James C. Mountflorence → the Secretary of State (Marshall/Madison)**, Paris, NARA RG 59 Consular Despatches Paris (T1 roll 1, frame 0097); catalogue 480 (class B) | 18 Mar 1801 | 26 Oct 2026 | complete | Key recovered from ciphertext-only: a one-part alphabetical code of some 600 groups, broken from four letter-spelled names (Lunéville, Parma, Tuscany, Dupont) and the order of the code. All 39 groups decoded: Spain has ceded to France "not Florida, but the Mississippi, with all its Dependencies", by secret articles at Lunéville, in compensation for Tuscany given to Parma. The Madison Papers had "key not found"; the decipherment predicted that Founders' 250 in TUSCANY should be 251, and the microfilm shows 251. Open: 4 word values (Mississippi, but, at, the) graded I | [`targets/mountflorence1801/`](targets/mountflorence1801/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/mountflorence1801.html) |
| **Juan Gara de Senisteros → Juan de la Peña**, Alcalá, Nationaal Archief 3.01.14 (Oldenbarnevelt) inv. 2442, early-17th-c. copy (found by the Nationaal Archief sweep, lead 13) | 23 Dec 1605 | 23 Oct 2026 | complete | Key recovered from ciphertext-only: vowels written as digits (4=a, 8=e, 3=i, 7=o, 2=u), consonants in clear, read off the first cipher line. About 97% of 1,447 cipher words read: the writer forged his patron's signature to win a canonry and rectorship; the sees of Sigüenza and Pamplona; a rumour about the Inquisitor Salcedo's ancestry. Open: ~46 scattered uncertain words | [`targets/senisteros1605/`](targets/senisteros1605/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/senisteros1605.html) |
| **An informant at Ernst of Bavaria's court → Duke Wilhelm V of Bavaria**, cipher in Latin letters, BayHStA Kurbayern Äußeres Archiv 4591 ff. 274–275 (DECODE R9424; catalogue 161; split from kaa4591 15 Oct 2026) | 12 Jun 1583 | 23 Oct 2026 | partial | **Key recovered from ciphertext-only by Ryan Xu** (ry-xu, with Claude Opus 5.5; issue #89, 23 Oct 2026) from this repository's transcription: a simple substitution on Latin letters, "/" doubles the previous letter, nulls at word edges; shuffled-key and annealing controls. Checked here on the DECODE images (23 Oct 2026): 9 of his 15 one-sign emendations settled by the image, the rest kept as supplied letters; the contemporary glosses on f. 275r agree (der, thumbrobst, Wirtzburg, Hildesheim). Read in part, 92.8% of words read as sense: the Cologne war three weeks after Ernst's election, rivalry among Ho., Fe. and Ni., "Susanna" to be brought down to Cologne or Liège, the Würzburg cathedral provostship. Open: the barred o-o nomenclator sign (3×), a name in l. 21, "eo" in l. 22, zodDcw3 (glossed Aichstet) | [`targets/r9424/`](targets/r9424/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/r9424.html) |
| **Huang Xing and the Shanghai group**, Tokyo ↔ Shanghai, 27 numeric telegrams in the 共密, 容密 and 辰密 codes, JACAR B03050089200–89500, B03050089900, B03050090000 (contributed) | 3 Jun – 2 Jul 1916 | 20 Oct 2026 | partial | **Reading audit, 22–23 Oct 2026** (George Lasry: a reading must build coherent sentences): read in part; the telegrams of all three codes read (2,100 of 2,100 groups). The holes inside them (21 + 47 marked groups, 6 illegible, 7 doubtful) keep it partial. **Keys recovered from ciphertext-only by Brice Dudout with Claude** (Oct 2026; [his pages](https://bdudout.github.io/wenmi/en/gongmi.html), email 19 Oct 2026; contributed): 共密 tens permutation then 9000 − C; 容密 per-place substitution + 0901; 辰密 the telegraph code reordered (zero-free page countdown). 1,940 of 2,100 groups without doubt; his verify.py rerun here and an independent decoder matches all 2,100; 154 doubtful, 6 illegible, V4B 18/22 sheets not re-read; 和密, 乾密 and N07 open | [`targets/numeric1916/`](targets/numeric1916/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/numeric1916.html) |

One homophonic key with word signs (che, con, et, per, qva, se) for both slips: 827 cipher signs, 780 assigned (94.3%), 721 give sense (87.2%, measured here); validation pass. f. 68: Count Francesco's obligations, "la secvreza nostra", a list of eight or ten trusted names; f. 70: a move against Cremona, Venice, date line "mediolani die qvarto mai". Open: 47 occurrences in 14 sign classes, 3 provisional values, the f. 70 signature. | **Hieronimo Ranzo's code**: the letter to "Garbino", Madrid, BnF fr. 3022 no. 20 ff. 44r–46v, and Ranzo's letters, fr. 2988 ff. 2, 9–10 (DECODE R1894), fr. 20506 ff. 136–138 (R4450, copy of ff. 9–10), fr. 3019 f. 73, fr. 4050 ff. 79–81, fr. 6638 pp. 67–70 (catalogue items 7, 170, 189, class C) | 11 Apr 1528 | 18 Sept 2026 | partial | **Reading audit, 22–23 Oct 2026** (George Lasry: a reading must build coherent sentences): read in part, 4,607 of 6,428 groups (0.717). Letter 4 (fr. 4050), joined into words on 23 Oct, went back to decoded, not read at verification (0.591); the other four letters read. Initial-letter code: each group is a word's initial with a number, not in alphabetical order (tested). **Key recovered from ciphertext-only by others, twice and independently, 6 Oct 2026**: **George Lasry** (with Claude Opus 5.5 and GPT-6 Astra, on Norbert Biermann's transcription of five letters; contributed 14 Oct 2026) and Paolo Rosson (with Claude, on this project's transcription). This project transcribed ~3,900 groups; its control-validated word annealer recovered only function words. Measured 18 Oct 2026 with Lasry's key first and Rosson's second (the keys agree on 94–96% of groups both value): **fr. 2988 f. 2 96.2%, ff. 9–10 97.0% (and the copy fr. 20506), over the 95% bar**, as Lasry wrote on 16 Oct 2026; no. 20 94.4%, fr. 4050 87.7%, fr. 6638 89.0%; 5,974 of 6,428 groups (92.9%). Open: scattered codes in fr. 2988; in letters 4–5, Lasry's tentative values that Rosson's key cannot confirm. Split from vasto1527 on 14 Oct 2026 | [`targets/ranzo1528/`](targets/ranzo1528/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/ranzo1528.html) |
| **Unknown sender → unknown recipient**, undated Latin diplomatic letter, Riksarkivet, Stockholm, Chifferklaver låda II:113 (DECODE R4282; separated from catalogue 207, class A) | Undated; catalogue 1600–1699 | 14 Oct 2026 | complete | **Contributed by George Lasry.** Transcribed with CTTS (CrypTool Transcriber and Solver); key recovered from ciphertext-only with the CTTS built-in homophonic solver. GPT-6 Astra improved and finalized the key and decryption using the images and four cleartext spans; no historical decipherment or external key was used. | **George W. Erving → James Madison**, Madrid, private No. 24 "Duplicate", "In Mr Pinkneys Cypher, the Cypher of ye Legation", NARA RG 59 Despatches from US Ministers to Spain vol. 10 = M31 reel 12 frames 0362–0367 (NAID 188605361; Founders Madison/99-01-02-1993, early access; not in the catalogue or on DECODE; sibling of [`targets/erving1807/`](targets/erving1807/)) | 10 Aug 1807 | 4 Oct 2026 | complete | **Key recovered from the Founders text and the code of the NARA duplicate; contributed by Feyseel Nur (PR #21), with Claude Opus 5.5 subagents; adversarial review by OpenAI Codex.** Founders prints the letter decoded, with ten blank gaps and without the groups; the duplicate on the film has the groups and no decode. 1,145 groups transcribed and aligned with the Founders text: Pinckney's legation code rebuilt to 414 rows, partly alphabetical (870–880 *their … thing*, 1340/1341/1343 *an/ance/and*). Checked against Jacob Wagner's 1803 decode of Pinckney's own despatch (M31 reel 7 frame 0348): 45 of 47 shared groups agree, 584 and 943 undecided. 1,125 of 1,145 groups (98.3%) give sense without the 11 conjectures (1,136 with them); Codex: confirmed but overstated, 716 groups (62.5%) have outside support (Wagner, the March letter), 908 (79.3%) recurrence or outside support, 0 digit errors in 53 sampled groups, and a conservative count without hidden-digit restorations and emendations of 1,081/1,147 (94.25%). Six of Founders' gaps filled from the code (*that government*, *and*, *hostile*, *resort*, *to Hanover and*, *these*), *expectations* plausible, *past* and *pride* guesses; three disagreements (*very [?] authority* for "best informed persons", *the vast [3 groups] Spain by the war* for "costs brought about", no *with*). Godoy paid for the Hanover bargain "out of his own funds", is "deceived and furious", and "as a dernier resort he will make an alliance of some sort with England!". Open: 633; 632, 455, 1482; 767, 679, 378; one group in the binding; 916 in the postscript; two uncertain positions on frame 0367. | [`targets/erving1807aug/`](targets/erving1807aug/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/erving1807aug.html) |

The four "uncertain codes" 48, 52, 65, 72 are digit pairs (4 8, 5 2, 6 5, 7 2) and two 26 are 2 6; the square-with-a-dot sign is the comte de Saint-Ibal (the clear instruction of 13 Apr on f. 21r asks that Santibal come with Mercy); "su camarero mayor" is Konrad von Burgsdorff, Brandenburg's chief chamberlain. Homophonic substitution with word division, two-stroke units (zo = u, pp = m, s3 = r, lz = d), polyphonic δ (u/b/g) and four word codes (Z = pour, 4 = roy, c+ = et, F = sieur); the transcription corrected against the images with the key (about 300 x→r fixes). A report on the Protestant rising in the Agenais a week after François II's death: 2,000 at Clairac, the Caumonts and their ministers, Biron turning back from Bergerac, the rebel captains at Nérac around Navarre ("le chief"), the Agenais deputies to the États of Orléans. The general [60] should join the regiments of Heister, Schneidau and Sporck, add 2,000 foot and camp at [64] so that it takes His Majesty's garrison, by force if need be: "ista nux durissima … frangi debet". Struck corrections: Wesselényi's draft. | **Ferdinand of Bavaria, Elector of Cologne (Bonn) → Duke Maximilian I of Bavaria**, ten ciphered pages, BayHStA, Kurbayern Äußeres Archiv 4591 ff. 281–286 (DECODE R9426, "non-decrypted", no author, no recipient, no place, no language) (catalogue 165, class C) | 22 Dec 1619 | 22 Sept 2026 | complete | (95.8% of cipher tokens measured). Broken ciphertext-only: a homophonic substitution on the even two-digit figures 10–78 (vowels with two or three homophones, consonants one, k and w only in the upper series), two signs for word division, and a three-digit nomenclator alongside; key recovered by annealing 2,482 letters against `lang/de-1500s`, with ~60 single-digit transcription slips repaired (0/6, 3/5, 1/4, 2/7) and a sample re-checked on the images. Control: the key reads the sibling R9425 unchanged ("Albrecht von Gottes Gnaden"); on 2 Oct 2026 R9425 (ff. 278–280) was transcribed and deciphered in full with the key unchanged, 97.3% of 1,567 cipher tokens measured: Archduke Albert's written resolution of 15 December 1619 for the League's agent Licentiate Morreus (Bohemia to be recovered, his troops to join the Catholic army, no muster place or 500 horse because his levies go to Luxembourg, Breisach referred to Oñate, the 1,000 cuirassiers to Ambrogio Spinola); it shows 414, 415, 416, 417, 419 to be nulls and fixes 242 = *und*, 165 = *Mt.*, 2 = *Ambrosio*. The record's "unknown" sender is named by the clear last page — Bonn, 22 December 1619, signed Ferdinand — and by the ciphered "mein Thumbcapitel", "meine Landtstendt", Paderborn. Six weeks after Frederick V's coronation: quartering companies in the county of Horn, at Montfort and at Deutz, two Kopfstück or two Ort of a Königsthaler a day, Lintelo's and Herberstorff's levies, a year's respite for Paderborn, and the argument that unless the Catholics act they will shortly have nothing left to secure them against "Einfal, Gwalt und Betrangnus". Not in *Briefe und Akten* NF I/1 or any other edition searched. Open: 31 of the 32 nomenclator groups (only 248 = *und* established; no key for this cipher survives in the volume), three unidentified names, four words | [`targets/ferdinand1619/`](targets/ferdinand1619/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/ferdinand1619.html) |

The four letters dated 1592 are 1593 new style (the year began on 25 March). **3 Oct 2026**: R7774 and R7775 re-read on the images (f. 109v lines 1–2 added, "[9]: of whome especiallie [o] hath made choise …"; f. 112 "carryinge"); every passage transcribed letter for letter and measured: **1,023 of 1,035 signs read (98.8%)**, 99.9% without the person signs. | Unknown writer → "Monsieur", Nancy, December (17th c.; signed "Nicle"?), TNA SP 106/10 ff. 241–243 (DECODE R927; catalogue 56, class B), "undeciphered letter in the masonic alphabet" | 17th c. | 19 Sept 2026 | partial | **Read bar, 24 Oct 2026** (a document counts as read only when 95% of its words read as sense): read in part; f. 241 reads at 90.1% of words, under 95%; f. 243 reads (95.4%). **Independent re-solution, not a first break** (Sheila Richards, *Secret Writings*, 1973, per Lasry). The pencil pigpen key on the flap (akt·blu·cmw/…) reads nothing in any orientation. The cipher is a homophonic pigpen (cell shape + 1–3 dots, inner-stroke variants; several signs for e, i, s, t, r). Hand transcription of the last page (450 signs) → quadgram solver finds French → key completed from words. Last page reads nearly throughout: *je ne pouvois recevoir d'un ennuy plus sensible, ayant appris que du mariage de ce gentilhomme … l'entiere affection que j'ay vouée a vostre service … et vous supplie tres humblement, monsieur, me continuer l'honneur de vostre bienveuillance et croire que je vous honnore*. **2 Oct 2026**: ff. 241 and 242 hand-transcribed and decoded. The letter runs **f. 243 → f. 241**; **f. 242 is a second copy of f. 243**, line for line. f. 241 closes it: an envoy to congratulate *Sa Majesté* on recovering from a long illness, *le zele que j'ay a son tres humble service*, signed "Nicle"(?), *De Nancy le [..] decembre*. Measured: **84.0% of the letter's signs read as sense** (f. 243 81.7%, f. 241 86.5%; the copy 73.5%); key extended (H3 = z, C*2 = q, C1 f/q and F2 l/h polyphones). Neighbouring records R922–R933 opened: none in this pigpen. **3 Oct 2026**: every sign of every unread word cut out at 3–4x and re-read (corner sides, dot counts, inner strokes, two writer-cancelled signs); F*1 = l, D*1 = o; a joint re-anneal over-fitted and was not adopted. f. 243 now reads *lequel salua dernierement [name] de la part de [name] mon maistre, vous aviés creu quelques choses au prejudice de l'entiere affection …* **843/882 signs read as sense (95.6%)**, f. 243 98.3%, f. 241 92.5%. Key rebuilt by matching the ciphertext against a plaintext found elsewhere (an edition, a calendar, a copy in another archive).

| **Philip IV → Cardinal Gil de Albornoz**, ambassador in Rome, Madrid, countersigned Pedro Coloma; Archivo Histórico de la Nobleza, Osuna, OSUNA,CT.2,D.32(1-2), copy A images 2–3, copy B image 5 (PARES 3939398); catalogue 747 (narrowed to the other shelfmarks; contributed by Brice Dudout) | 24 Jul 1648 | 27 Oct 2026 | complete | **Key recovered from external plaintext by Brice Dudout** (with Claude Opus 5.5; [his write-up](https://bdudout.github.io/europe-chiffree/en/documents.html#albornoz-1648); contributed): a syllabic cipher, syllables written with the consonant changed and the vowel kept (mo = co, ne = de, ra = sa), numbers in series of five (45–49 ra…ru), single letters (2 = n, e = n, n = s, m = r) and code signs (✗ = que, sal = Su Santidad). Key K2 rebuilt from another paper in the same cipher, Luis de Haro to the Count of Peñaranda, Madrid, 25 Sept 1649, enciphered for Albornoz, and its plain copy of the time (OSUNA,CT.11,D.1, PARES 3939336): learnt on ll. 1–12 and frozen; held-out ll. 13–23 give 0.859 of letters (200 permuted-value keys: median 0.419, p99 0.476); 29 values added on the letter (K2b). The royal cipher of 1644–45 (K1, rebuilt from CT.2,D.16 and tested on D.20, 0.638 against p99 0.550) does not fit. Validation pass (636 groups; −2.038 against −3.743 for random keys and −2.565 for a shuffled refit). Both copies agree; 353 of 359 groups (98.3%). The King insists that Giulio Rospigliosi, nuncio at Madrid and the future Clement IX, be kept: *no puede haver instrumento más a propósito*. R1138 and its second version (Vestigia 1920) deciphered by W. Somogyi (*Scriptorium* VI, 2025). (2 Oct 2026) No. 12 of 25 Feb 1521 (Vestigia 1921, not on DECODE) deciphered, 161 of 166 signs (97.0 %): the custode blamed for the claim that Eger was given away before the new bishop came; five key values added (lone o = h/u, oo = b, crossed z/x = q); second transcriptions at full resolution bring R1139 to 88.7 % and R1136 to 66.9 % of decoded letters; 3 Oct 2026 (passes 5–6, look-alike table and pen shapes): R1139 89.7 % ("mi per Milan"), R1136 unchanged; 992 of 1,236 over the three letters (80.3 %). Split 14 Oct 2026 (one key per entry): Caprile's 1519 two-tier cipher (R1128) and Cistarelli's figure cipher (R1137) have their own rows under Not solved. | [`targets/caprile1520/`](targets/caprile1520/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/caprile1520.html) |

### Key recovered based on adjacent plaintext

Key rebuilt from plaintext beside the ciphertext: a partial interlinear or marginal decipherment, clear passages, a deciphered copy in the same file.

| Target | Date | Landed | Extent | Result | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

July 1729 | 28 Oct 2026 | complete | One-part alphabetical French code (1–1370); table of 379 values rebuilt from the interlinear decipherments on five sibling letters of the same agent (942 pairs), gaps bracketed by alphabetical order: 134 of 141 groups. The agent complains that Keene leaves him without orders during the accommodation. Open: 6, 274, 254, 937, 1052, 726, 836. | [`targets/portstemarie1729/`](targets/portstemarie1729/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/portstemarie1729.html) |

Key K1710 rebuilt by crib from a ciphered troop list and its clear draft in the same file (fols. 73/82 against 74), frozen and tested on unseen letters (fol. 100, fols. 78v–79r: 93% and 96% of words recognised, control keys at most 80%). Both copies deciphered, 639 of 640 groups; group 212 open, ten word values set from the table's order and the sense, five clerk's slips. After Helsingborg the King offers Denmark peace under the Maritime Powers' mediation and gives the Council a free hand to agree an armistice, "even for a whole year". Statistical validation underdetermined (178 numbers for 638 occurrences: a key fitted to shuffled numbers scores as well as the reading). The Council's clear copy, probably in the Riksarkivet, not consulted. | [`targets/stade1710/`](targets/stade1710/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/stade1710.html) |

Twelve residual I tokens, g121 if gemelte is retained, and unread s439; unglossed siblings remain workable. Blind test M 10/10, I 3/6, not cleanly blind. | **Snell → an unidentified correspondent ("A?ud")**, invisible-ink message copied for the Hessian authorities, HStAM 9 a Nr. 259 f. 249 (HCPortal record 513, "polyalphabetic", not solved; catalogue 340, C) | 20 Feb 1824 | 28 Sept 2026 | complete | **Key rebuilt from the decipherer's note on the leaf; whole text read.** The note gives the key letters bcdefg and the first word; the table is a Vigenère without j (b..g = +2..+7) whose columns run on past z into the signs 1–4 ("de 4ette" = de Wette). 169 signs, one omitted sign and 11 copy slips corrected. Questions about Paul Follen, America, Fries, Oken, de Wette and "the discovery of this plan in Wezlar". | John Quincy Adams → Secretary of State, St Petersburg, No. 88, 25 June 1812 (Ford, Writings 4:357, "[Nine lines of this paragraph not decyphered.]"), NARA M35 reel 3 frames 0206–0207 | 1812 | 22 Sept 2026 | partial | **Read bar, 24 Oct 2026** (a document counts as read only when 95% of its words read as sense): read in part; the remainder of No. 88 reads at 93.8% of words, under 95%; the nine lines Ford left undeciphered read (98.0%). **Solved: premise refuted, code rebuilt, read** (22 Sept 2026, third pass: **380 of 390 groups read as sense, 0.974, measured**; Ford's nine lines 133/133, the remainder 247/257 = 0.961). Adams's despatches are not in Armstrong's THE = 972 code, as the Madison editors state: every coded despatch on M35 reel 3 (and reel 2 frame 0155) has the = 1385, of = 1576, that = 1384, and = 668; he held Armstrong's cypher only to write to Paris (Ford 3:328, 3:370). His own code rebuilt to 1,166 numbers from the clerk's interlinear decodes (frames 0047, 0048, 0182–0185, 0189, 0190, 0194, 0198, 0206, 0210–0211, 0247) and Ford's printed decipherments of 1811–1812 blocks (No. 95 aligned against the Department's clear copy). The nine lines: the journey to Wilna went against the Chancellor's advice, Rumyantsev's apoplectic stroke soon after reaching Wilna, a second and worse one before 25 June, a false report of his death in the German gazettes. The remainder (frame 0207): Kurakin's note on the evacuation of Prussia, which Lauriston called a proposal "France had never thought of making to Russia even after the battle of Friedland"; Napoleon expected to strike at the centre of the empire rather than along the shores of the Baltic. Open: 7 groups cut by the binding gutter on the film (needs the original), 3 single code groups (Adams's copying slips 1112 1433 read as intended, 'impaired') | [`targets/jqa/`](targets/jqa/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/jqa.html) |
| Court informant → Louis de Gonzague, duc de Nevers, Paris, BnF fr. 3976 f. 62 (DECODE R3705; sibling ff. 131, 133 same informant; catalogue item 187, class C) | 29 Apr 1588 | 22 Sept 2026 | complete | (350 of 353 plaintext letters of the 19 cipher runs, measured) with a key rebuilt from the interlinear glosses of the same informant's letter of 4 June 1588 (f. 131): Henri III 'cuyda estre resolu au conseil de prendre et jetter en l'eau cinq ou six des plus grans ligueurs de' Paris, urged by the Queen, the chancellor and d'O, held back by Villeroy's remonstrances; Guise's answer that he had '40 mil hommes'. The name in run 1 (Fougères?) and code numbers 28, 49, 70 open (10 = Épernon, glossed on f. 139). The key's source letter, f. 131 (4 June 1588), was itself deciphered in full on 2 Oct 2026 with the key: 130 of 130 cipher signs and 8 of 8 code numbers (9 Guise, 10 Épernon, 11 le Roy, 24 Nevers, 72 Savoie, 73 ambassadeur), every run agreeing with its gloss of the time (G04 is "que s'il perissoit", not "pouvoit"): the Hôtel de Ville assembly moving to an apology to Henri III, the Brissac/Elbeuf rumours at Rouen, Épernon at Loches, and Savoy arming to take the marquisate of Saluzzo. | [`targets/nevers1588/`](targets/nevers1588/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/nevers1588.html) |

(21 Sept 2026). A clear despatch with five lines in a substitution of invented signs, no decipherment; broken from the crib "audensier" in the clear text. The Audiencier and Luigi Marliano expect the King's entry into Brussels at the end of the week, the Emperor to go to Cambrai about the 15th (L&P II 2862 summarised only the Cambrai clause). Word signs for the Emperor and (probably) the King read from context. | [`targets/spinelly1516/`](targets/spinelly1516/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/spinelly1516.html) |

The key then reads the three election letters whose "déchiffrement" is only that cut gloss: 1 May to the King, *ceste nation est autant vénale et sujette à se laisser gaigner par argent comme sont les Allemans*; 1 May to Anjou, *l'Empereur en y a despendu plus de trois cens mil … faisant le pis qu'il peult contre vous*; 9 May from Płock, the election carried *contre la volunté et menées du Grand Seigneur, de l'Empereur, des princes de l'Empire, du Roy d'Espaigne, du Moscovite et du Roy de Suède, qui tous estoient bandez contre vostre Majesté*. Tomokiyo's table for the cipher corrected. (2 Oct 2026) The two long letters of 24 April re-transcribed at full scale and deciphered with the key, extended by about a dozen signs: to Anjou (f. 154r 96.7 %, 154v 92.0 %), the Lithuanian vote and the Sultan's recommendation made *avec ruse*; to Catherine (f. 156r 97.2 %, 156v 88.2 %), *vous serez mere des deux plus grans roys du monde*, Denmark and the Sound, money and galleys; 1,521 of 1,639 signs (92.8 %) at that pass. (3 Oct 2026) Every open group re-read at 2x against Tomokiyo's current table (st, il, nous, par, t adopted) and ff. 160, 164, 174 re-transcribed word-aligned: every cipher letter is over the read bar, measured per letter: f. 124 96 %, to Anjou 97.5 %, to Catherine 95.8 % (the cipher shows the German princes *se monstrent estre ennemis jurez de … France*, where the decipherer wrote "si monsieur estoit ennemy"), f. 160 100 %, f. 164 98.1 % (*car si jusques cy … a despendu trente mil escus*), f. 174 100 %; 2,094 of 2,159 signs (97.0 %). Open: 65 signs at the microfilm gutter or under overwriting (Gallica has one microfilm only), single signs, and one word sign on f. 164 that is in no key. The four 9 May letters ff. 182–189 are in clear. | [`targets/lanssac/`](targets/lanssac/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/lanssac.html) |
| García de Toledo → Philip II, Messina, 16 July 1565, AGS Estado leg. 1394 no. 247 (*duplicado de carta cifrada*; catalogue item 33) | 1565 | 18 Sept 2026 | complete | (18 Sept 2026): a homophonic substitution on two-digit figures 12–43, in plain alphabetical order (a 12–14, b 15, c 16 … u/v 38–40, x 41, y 42, z 43; vowels three figures each, consonants one, no nulls and no nomenclator), used inline in otherwise-clear Spanish; 1,930 figure groups read end to end from the PARES images. Two runs beside clear words broke it — *seiscientos* after "los", *en tiera* after "puso" — and the alphabetical order then predicted the unseen values (f 21, x 41, y 42, z 43), each confirmed on *effetos*, *baxeles*, *yban*, *plazer*. The viceroy of Sicily, a week after the Piccolo Soccorso got into Birgu, reports a **second relief run waved off**: the galleys came within four miles of the harbour mouth, the *barqueta* sent ahead arranged the fire-signals, and La Valette signalled *clarissimas* that they must not enter, twice within the appointed hour, so they slipped back out to sea undetected — as *Mosiur de Lenni* (Andrea Provana di Leinì) and the rest report, with Çanoguera piloting. Also: La Valette embracing Cardona's 600 soldiers and weeping, unable to speak; the forbidden sortie that killed 200 Turks; Turkish lines "seven, eight and ten palms high"; 1,500 Turks at the water and sickness killing men in the Ottoman camp; the Algiers squadron off Cabo Bono; 40,000 escudos a month for the ships, half the 48,000 ducados already eaten, and *El estar sin galeras me destruye*. The companion covering letter of the same day, AGS Estado leg. 1394 no. 249 (PARES 3576586, *duplicado*), was deciphered in full on 2 Oct 2026 with the key unchanged, 150 of 150 cipher tokens (two new signs: barred θ = *ll*, an overlined M-like sign = *r*): La Valette's latest letters are *harto mas apretado* than expected, the Algiers ships off *Cabo Bono*, the galleys for *la Goleta* held for the *respuesta del Maestre* and leaving *mañana*. Tomokiyo does not list it and PARES records no decipherment; the non-duplicate originals of 247 and 249 and the Mdina governor's enclosure are still to be found | [`targets/toledo1565/`](targets/toledo1565/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/toledo1565.html) |

| Lope Hurtado de Mendoza (Rome) → Charles V, nine letters, Sept–Nov 1522, RAH Salazar 9/26 (DECODE R9634, R9644–R9646, R9648–R9650, R9652, R9656; catalogue 144, class B) | Sept–Nov 1522 | 20 Sept 2026 | partial | **Reading audit, 22–23 Oct 2026** (George Lasry: a reading must build coherent sentences): read in part, 89 of 2,104 transcribed tokens (0.042). Of the letters with a token transcription only R9649 reads; R9634, R9645, R9646 and R9656, re-read on the images, are decoded, not read (0.51–0.73 clean). R9644, R9648 and R9650 read from their contemporary clear versions. **Cipher broken and the key rebuilt; four letters read complete as to content.** Bound among Alonso Sánchez's Venice letters and catalogued with them, but a **different, unpublished cipher** — Sánchez's groups end strictly in `b c d f g h l m n`, Hurtado's take finals `p`/`z`, and `xul` is *despues* in Sánchez against *lo* here; Tomokiyo's 2025 article covers Sánchez and Juan Manuel, not this. The crib was bound with it: **R9644 and R9650 each carry the clerk's own full contemporary clear version** beside the cipher, keyed by marginal letters A/B/C. **47 code values fixed** against that plaintext, 29 probable (kept apart; an audit produced 4 retractions, and the 10 substitution-alphabet signs were later downgraded to probable when an 8× zoom showed signs that must be different letters looking near-identical at the magnification they were read at — the code groups, plain latin trigrams that cross-check, are unaffected). The key reads where it was not built: 70%/62%/57% of tokens in R9648/R9650/R9645, and *el papa* unaided in R9656, which has no crib. Structure not in the published account of the siblings: homophones at code level (`ta`/`tu` both *no*), codes carrying syllables (*na·da*, *que·da·rian*, *con·ocia*) and codes running together (*porque*, *quales*). Read complete: R9644, R9648 (its duplicate), R9650, R9652 — Adrian VI temporising between Charles V and Francis I, the French into Italy with a great sum to Lyon, Adorno's intercepted letters, the Pope *tan escasso que nunca hombre lo fue mas*. **Correction to DECODE**: R9652 carries no cipher at all. **2 Oct 2026**: R9649 deciphered in full from its own clear (f. 268; the Ostia fortress, the licenciado Bernardino); the key extended on **R9634, which is from Genoa, 13 Sept 1522** (DECODE has the month only): Geronimo Adorno's plan for Charles V and Henry VIII to invade France through **Provence**, *la tierra mas flaca que el tiene*, 70,000 ducats a month, set for March — 846/1191 tokens read as sense (71%); then R9645 73% (f. 243r = the Ferrara capitulation terms), R9646 80%, R9656 61%, all measured. **3 Oct 2026**: codes pooled over all 21 Hurtado letters, spelled words matched against a Spanish lexicon, R9656 re-transcribed from the images: R9634 872/1191 (73.2%), R9645 75.3%, R9656 177/225 (78.7%), 76% over the five; still partial (DECODE has no larger image; the RAH Biblioteca Digital is behind a bot check). New values: `ɋ` = r, plural `z` after codes, `yob` ducado, `xu` gente, nulls after the opener `Ꮒ`. Open: about 40 single-occurrence code groups in R9634 (no crib, not in CSP Spain ii) and spelled words with one sign at the limit of DECODE's resolution in all four partial letters | [`targets/lopehurtado/`](targets/lopehurtado/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/lopehurtado.html) |

### Read after matching with key from external sources

An existing key not previously tied to the document was found to fit and applied.

| Target | Date | Landed | Extent | Result | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Filippo Maria Visconti, Duke of Milan → Francesco Sforza, the cipher slips of his last winter**, Milan, Abbiategrasso and Cusago, BnF italien 1583 ff. 110–158 (DECODE R7902, R7904, R7907, R7914, R7915) and italien 1584 ff. 5–102 (Gallica); Oct 2026 Venice/Milan sweep leads 1307 and 1312 | 1 Nov 1446 – 23 Mar 1447 | 29 Oct 2026 | partial | Key identified on DECODE: an anonymous 18th-c. Milanese copy headed "Filippo Maria Duca di Milano" (ASMi Carteggio Sforzesco cart. 1597 no. 23, R5323; a second copy is R1857 image 17). It agrees with the two contemporary translations at 96.9% and 96.5%. 19 slips transcribed, 15,033 of 16,637 signs read (90.4%). Sixteen slips have a decipherment of the time or a later copy: every ital. 1584 letter on the next leaves (decipherments ff. 6, 9, 25, 38, 84, 95, 103; copies), and ital. 1583 ff. 110 and 136 in 19th-c. copies in ital. 1597, made by deciphering the slips. Only ital. 1583 ff. 130, 154, 158 have no clear text reached (80-89%). Corrected 29 Oct 2026: the first write-up counted nine slips without clear text; the blind readings of ff. 23/24, 72, 102 agree with their clear texts on 83-88% of words. ff. 110 and 136 merged from a parallel session. The key heading, subject and diplomatic role make Philippe Groffey the probable unsigned writer. Both cipher pages were then transcribed afresh from the images, group by group (30 Sept, 5 Oct 2026): 982 of 1,017 groups (96.6%) read as sense (`measure_tr.py`), twelve after a one-figure correction forced by context. The *Zifra Prima* match stands. **Deciphered in part with the Council of Ten's *Zifra Prima* of 1577–78 (ASVe CX Cifre b. 4 reg. 16 ff. 64–65, DECODE R1789), plus two homophones found from context (d83 = a, c86 = o): 981 of 1,565 cipher groups (62.7%, measured).** The first-pass transcription (130-dpi scan) misread 7 as 2, 8 as 0 and confused 3/5/8; corrected by hand (721 groups, 46.0%), then by a noisy-channel decoder calibrated on the hand reading (89.3% agreement on held-out pages) and by fitting proposed lines, with a wrong-line control (10% of groups fit by chance vs 80% on the true lines). Two pieces: the Senate to Mocenigo on the French ambassador's request that Venice help Henry III and move the Emperor to aid him; and the copy of the Senate's answer to the French ambassador (de Maisse): Venice will intercede with Sixtus V to quiet the risings and "ammorzar l'incendio di guerra". Open: ff. 35v–36v (29–48% read) and gaps on ff. 37r–38r, digits the scan does not separate; needs a 400-dpi BNE scan, the Senate register (ASVe Deliberazioni Secreta 1589) or de Maisse's letter-book (BL Add MS 30635–37) | [`targets/ottobon/`](targets/ottobon/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/ottobon.html) |

Telegram received; glad to hear from you; wrote by this morning's post; quite well and writing by post; weather very fine, sea quite smooth. *Promulgo* is the book's "Put me £1,000 to win on —", which the letter does not explain; *Bert* is plain text. The letter covers the French attack on Roussillon, Fuenterrabía, Guipúzcoa and Galicia, Henry VII's treaty duty to help, and a request for *dos mil peones* (not the marriage). The 3 Oct clear copy in PTR 53/72 summarises it. | Conde de Miranda → duque de Feria; duque de Sessa → Diego de Ibarra; Sessa → Philip II, Feb–Jun 1593, BnF fr. 3983 nos. 45, 79 and fr. 3984 no. 47, the Spanish office cipher of 1592–93 (catalogue item 19, class B; no. 68 split off 14 Oct 2026) | 1593 | 19 Sept 2026 | partial | **Reading audit, 22–23 Oct 2026** (George Lasry: a reading must build coherent sentences): read in part, 982 of 1,310 groups (0.75). Nos. 79 and 47 read; no. 45 went back to decoded, not read at verification (0.50 clean, 23 Oct). **The Spanish office's own key survives, with its nomenclature, in another volume.** The catalogue cited piece numbers; the BnF *dépouillement* gives folios and the leaves were found on the images — **ff. 98, 162, 108 = canvases 178, 286, 198**. Tomokiyo had reconstructed the key and published it **as an image** a text dump drops (`phelippes2.png`); no reading of these leaves was published. His table has the syllables but **no nomenclature**. Following the entry's own *"verify first: DECODE for Sessa"*: DECODE's only two hits for these correspondents (**records 4076, 4077**) are **keys**, both naming **BnF fr. 3995**. **Folio 97 there is the office key, complete**: alphabet, syllable table (identical to Tomokiyo's) and the full nomenclature (*causa* ʃes, *estados* vul, *mucho* hur, *negocios* norî, *Rey* tas, *Roma* vos…). Diacritics confirmed on plaintext: ring = −h− (*achaque*), dot = final −l (*bolsa*). The decode **agrees with the 1593 decipherer's own interlinear words on no. 47**. Every cipher line re-transcribed and scored for sense (5 Oct 2026): **1,212 of 1,310 groups (92.5%)**: no. 45 90.6%, no. 79 92.3%, no. 47 93.7%. *con este achaque, que siendo confidente … a costa de la bolsa*; *los que bivimos en Roma*; Desportes, Pisani, Gondi. **Open:** 22 code groups not on the f. 97 sheet, about 75 doubtful syllable groups. The fourth letter of the item, Ibarra's from Paris (no. 68), is in the Feria–Mansfeld cipher and has its own row. | [`targets/sessa1593/`](targets/sessa1593/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/sessa1593.html) |

| **Ferdinand I (Augsburg) → Giovanni Maria (Johann) Malvezzi (Constantinople)**, Budapest, Hadtörténelmi Levéltár 1548/3 (DECODE R366, "partially decrypted", homophonic with graphic signs; catalogue item, class B) | 23 Jan 1548 | 21 Sept 2026 | complete | The key was on DECODE itself, filed at the same shelfmark as a separate record (R367, with a second copy of the alphabet as R386). Mostly clear Latin with about three pages of cipher, and the cipher is where the politics is: the French are trying in many deceitful ways to break the Habsburg–Ottoman peace, for no other reason than that, not daring to attack Charles V with their own forces, they want it done at the Sultan's charges; Rüstem Pasha is not to doubt Habsburg sincerity. The money passage (pp. 5–6) reads almost continuously: 2,800 ducats of arrears and 1,500 for the year from 1 March, carried by the secretary Justus de Argento, plus a credit of up to 3,000 repayable at Venice against Malvezzi's note. Nulls are whole Latin words (*etiam*, *idcirco*, *Porro*). Second pass (5 Oct 2026): every open stretch re-read from the full-resolution images with five variant sign forms of the hand; all five runs now read, 509 of 514 cipher words (99.0%) measured as Latin sense (p. 6 foot: the troops on the border; p. 7: the captives). Open: the banker's surname after *Gulielmo* (signs give *Semaslia*), one two-sign group, two lone *v* signs. The letter is probably in print in *Austro-Turcica 1541–1552* (1995), not consulted. | [`targets/malvezzi1548/`](targets/malvezzi1548/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/malvezzi1548.html) |

| Charles Hémard de Denonville, bishop and then cardinal of Mâcon (Rome, Orvieto) → Anne de Montmorency, his letters in Mascon's cipher: BnF Français 3053 ff. 6, 16, 21, 32, 35, 40, 77 (DECODE R4233, R4234, R4235, R4238, R4239, R4240, R4247; catalogue 180, class A) and BnF fr. 3071 no. 4 ff. 9–10 (catalogue item 6) | Sept 1536 – 11 Apr 1537 | 21 Sept 2026 | partial | **One key, one entry (14 Oct 2026): R4248, in a second fr. 3053 cipher read from its f. 86 decipherment, is now its own entry (r4248); the cardinal's letter of 11 Apr 1537 (fr. 3071 no. 4, read 18 Sept 2026 under gramont1529: the Farnese marriage, Paul III's threat to declare for the Emperor, the Turk; its f. 9v passage confirmed by the contemporary slip f. 9bis) moved here, same key.** The “unknown sender” identified and every cipher passage of the seven fr. 3053 records deciphered in part. The catalogue had eight letters to the grand maître from an unnamed correspondent at Rome. Three are signed in clear *Charles e. de Mascon* / *E. Card[ina]l de Mascon*: the volume is the despatches of Francis I's ambassador to Paul III. The cipher is the one Tomokiyo reconstructed from fr. 3053 itself and Lasry re-tabulated in 2023 (used in `targets/gramont1529/`), with **no plaintext published anywhere**, so the work was reading. Homophonic substitution on graphic signs, doubled-letter signs, two nulls and exactly three code numbers (20 = l'Empereur, 30 = pape, 40 = roy — no other number occurs). Read: R4240 ~85% (Vergerio's message to the Germans, the Pope's volte-face on the general council), R4247 letter 1 ~80% (Andrea Doria's galleys, a suspected secret treaty with the Emperor, Milan refused to the Farnese), R4238 ~65% (the Sienese exiles, a German offering Ferdinand's secrets), R4233 ~60% (the Farnese marriages, a conclave weighed while Paul III fails, a design on Milan), passages of R4235 (*le dict pape vouloit entretenir le roy de mariage … pour ce s'en prevaloir vers le dict Empereur*) and of R4239 (Ottavio Farnese's match with Margaret of Austria, the Florentine exile cardinals, an *honneste estat en France* offered to Pier Luigi Farnese against the Imperial offer of Novara), and 66 lines of R4234. Six contemporary marginal or interlinear decipherments found in the volume served as controls, three agreeing word for word; one of them corrects the published table (the big curly X is Q/U, not N). A third pass the same day read every remaining page: the rest of R4234 (Pier Luigi's troops held in Parma and Piacenza to garrison Milan if the Emperor sells it; Ambroise *corruptible plus que tous les hommes*), R4235 P2-P3 and passage D (against a bound clear copy), R4247's second letter (Pitigliano: strike at Naples) and the 100 unread lines of R4239 (Novara offered at the Pope's command). About three quarters of the cipher letters were then read. **Pass 5 (22 Sept)**: the readers' one barred-cross alias proved to be four or five distinct key signs; every record re-transcribed by sign shape from the Gallica native scans and decoded with the key + a French LM, checked against every clear copy (ff. 37r-v found to be the clear copy of R4239 letter 2; Dupuy 44 companion letter to the King): **~95 % of all cipher letters read**, CER 3-8 % vs contemporary clear texts; R4247's 'faded' tail is sharp on Gallica; 'Lodes' is Lodi, not Rodez. Open: ~5 % ambiguous sign runs, some names; in fr. 3071 no. 4 seven signs on f. 9r ll. 27-28 and a name | [`targets/rome1536/`](targets/rome1536/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/rome1536.html) |

Unknown nomenclator usage falls from 11.0 % to 3.4 %. Two corrections to the filed key: 4474 is *Vicerè*, not a bishop, and 4535 "Apte" is *Aprile*. Stato Portogallo 117 items 1–8 (DECODE R25, R195–R201, "partially decrypted"; catalogue item 240, class A) | 1758–60 | 19 Sept 2026 | complete | **Reading audit, 22–23 Oct 2026** (George Lasry: a reading must build coherent sentences): all eight letters read (8,326 of 8,326 cipher tokens). | Nicolas Raince → Montmorency, Rome, 13 May and 20 Nov 1526, BnF fr. 2984 pp. 29–31 and pp. 105–106 (catalogue item 5, class C) | 1526 | 17 Sept 2026 | partial | **27 Oct 2026: the 20 Nov letter read by Brice Dudout** (pull request #163): the lower 21 lines of p. 105 and the whole of p. 106, a page of cipher missed here, about 98% of some 610 words with Tomokiyo's table as re-measured here, on the full-size Gallica images; the 13 May letter (pp. 29-31) stays decoded, not read, and can be re-read the same way. **Reading audit, 22–23 Oct 2026** (George Lasry: a reading must build coherent sentences): not read; the key method stands (restored 24 Oct 2026, George Lasry: key and text are separate) (was read in part, read with known key). With Tomokiyo's 1526 key re-measured, the passages decode but clauses break in nearly every line; 0.87 (letters in words) is withdrawn as a reading figure. **86 of about 106 lines deciphered** (17 Sept 2026). Not a cryptanalytic problem: Tomokiyo published the 1526 key in 2020 with no plaintext, but reading which glyph sits under which letter in his table *image* by eye slips a column — the first pass here had l, m and n each one place wrong and H under r, which corrupted every reading built on it. Re-measured off the image (header letters located, ink blobs assigned to the nearest column, every one within 15 px of a centre), the table resolves a control line glyph for glyph with nothing left over: *s [null] qui estoit en la court de Savoye [null] est party pour venir icy*. 86 of the 106 lines then read by hand off the microfilm against numbered-token images, gaps left as gaps; 20 lines carry a machine decode only. The 13 May letter, nine days before the League of Cognac: the Castilians and the Bourguignons, *deliberé prendre le chemin de Valence*, a capitulation for which *ilz seront cause de la destruction … de la part d'avoir faict capituler*, *mondict seigneur l'Admiral luy avoit dict que seroit celuy qui y tiendroit*, *la peste qui est au logis*, *des advis qui viennent aux imperiaulx par leurs*, and a man taken *un jour, en plaine place pres du palais, un nommé Pietro Anthonio*. Its clear close, transcribed here, expects Andrea Doria at Civitavecchia within a day with six galleys. The 20 Nov letter, two months after the Colonna raid, turns on a pontificate that *seroit la cause de la totale ruine de sa maison*. One glyph, ten occurrences, always at a clause boundary, is in no published table. Gallica leaf numbers run two apart because they are page numbers on odd pages, not because half the volume is unfilmed | [`targets/raince/`](targets/raince/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/raince.html) |

No. 92 is decoded, not read: holes in most cipher sentences; 'about three quarters of the words' was an estimate by line. The *Cifra general* key and its check on eight glosses stand. **Deciphered with the *Cifra general de los Reyes Católicos*** (Galende Díaz 1994, app. 1; assigned by Tomokiyo), proved here against eight interlinear glosses with no misses; both pages transcribed and about three quarters of the words deciphered (estimate). The Great Captain's price for taking the fleet against the Turk in 1500: harbours in Italy for his sick, wounded and stores; Sanuto's *Diarii* report the same demand. Open: five code groups that contradict the printed key (BRAH 9/15 not digitised) and about ten letter-sign stretches | [`targets/esp318/`](targets/esp318/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/esp318.html) |
| Nevers → Pisany, 8 Sept and 14 Oct 1593, BnF fr. 3985 f. 209 and fr. 3986 f. 168, cipher no. 46 (catalogue item 21, part) | 1593 | 17 Sept 2026 | none (key obtained, text not read) | **Reading audit, 22–23 Oct 2026** (George Lasry: a reading must build coherent sentences): not read; the key method stands (restored 24 Oct 2026, George Lasry: key and text are separate) (was read in part, read with known key). f. 209 (0.33 on explicit holes) and f. 168 are decoded, not read: key no. 46 values 390 of 391 figure groups, but the values do not build sentences. **Both Pisany letters deciphered, 390 of 391 figure groups take a value** (17 Sept 2026). Tomokiyo's Nevers cipher no. 46 (fr. 3995 f. 87, read from the full-resolution Gallica image; two-digit alphabet with homophones, barred/plain/dotted word lists, 1 as an intercalated null, checked against the office's decipherment of a Gondi letter in the same key) resolves every figure of both Pisany letters: 8 Sept reads whole ("l'on dissuadoit [le Roy] de rechercher [que le Pape] luy donnast l'absolution … qu'il n'estoyt besoin qu'il allast sans cela, parce qu'il ne feroit rien … l'on n'a volonté de contanter le Pape, que l'on n'y aille poynt"), 14 Oct in long stretches, garbled where the copy's figures are misread. The letters to Revol are in the Court's symbol cipher no. 60, not no. 46, and are their own entry since 14 Oct 2026 (`targets/revol1593/`) | [`targets/nevers1593/`](targets/nevers1593/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/nevers1593.html) |
| Catherine de Médicis and the Court → Bernardin Bochetel, bishop of Rennes, ambassador at the imperial court: BnF français 3181 f. 55; Cinq cents Colbert 390 pp. 139, 357; Colbert 392 p. 231 (catalogue 11, class B) | Dec 1562 – 1564 | 20–21 Sept 2026 | none (key obtained, text not read) | **Reading audit, 22–23 Oct 2026** (George Lasry: a reading must build coherent sentences): not read; the key method stands (restored 24 Oct 2026, George Lasry: key and text are separate) (was read in part, read with known key). Verification on 23 Oct put f. 55, pp. 357–358 and Colbert 392 p. 231 back to decoded, not read (0.50, 0.27 and 0.17 clean sentences), with p. 139 already so; Tomokiyo's key and the per-hand calibration stand. **Four letters never deciphered, read in part (79% of 2,308 signs measured as sense, 15% probable; 5 Oct 2026).** Bishop of Rennes’ cipher (Tomokiyo’s reconstruction): homophonic, ~30 word-signs, clear-looking word nulls. Each letter is in a different secretary’s hand, so each hand’s forms were fixed on letters in that hand that carry contemporary decipherments (fr. 3181 f. 57–58; Colbert 390 p. 138, pp. 189/199, 221–231), and each line decoded by a language-model lattice over the candidate values (`lattice.py`, `fr-1530-despatches`). **f. 55** (Catherine, 31 July 1563; La Ferrière II 79–81 printed the cipher as *« Partie chiffrée »*): ~91%, the Council of Trent and the Habsburg marriage — *pour le bien de la Chrestienté et avancer le concile… ce qui se promect des sessions de Decembre… vous estant dernierement à … Trante*; f. 57’s margin reads *par France*, not *par Ferrare*. **Colbert 390 pp. 357–358** (1564, after Ferdinand I’s death; p. 358 carries the end of the cipher): ~99% — drop the precedence quarrel on another pretext, *laisser là quelque secretaire… soubz couleur d’aucuns voz affaires particuliers*. **Colbert 392 p. 231** (Bourdin, Paris, Dec 1562; clear end on p. 232): ~85% — encloses Catherine’s letter of 15 Dec 1562 (La Ferrière I 448–451) on the secret marriage overture, which *sçavoit incontinant en Espaigne*; *[s]a femme … triumphe de la premiere ouverture… de ce mariage*. **Colbert 390 p. 139** (*Deschiffrez vous mesmes… et la mettez au feu*): ~65%, three code words for persons/places unread — *que je vous tienne pour trop advisé et affectionné et loyal serviteur du roy mon fils… les yeux ouverts pour observer… mon cousin*. Calibration drew on every decipherment found in Colbert 390–395 (full ones on pp. 120–121, 199, 313). Open: signs that occur in no decipherment (f. 55 ~9%, pp. 357–358 ~1%, Colbert 392 ~15%, p. 139 ~35%, faint and null-heavy); none of the four is printed | [`targets/rennes1563/`](targets/rennes1563/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/rennes1563.html) |

No. 26 is not a second letter but the same letter of 13 February, carrying the same cipher with the plaintext written between the lines by a contemporary decipherer; the clear text of the two copies runs word for word, the cipher stands at the same points, and no. 25's first run opens `to 92 … 17 … 43` and its third ends `… 68 93 …` exactly as no. 26's does. The letter reads in full: the Legate's departure for Spain and "le Pape pour l'acheminement de ces troupes in la Valteline", the fear "sy le Pape entroit dans la partialité"; the peace with the Huguenots, needed because "le faix d'une double guerre ne se pouvoit plus supporter"; the Dutch ships withdrawn and the Huguenot admiral following "avec le reste de sa flotte" without the King's permission; and the prince of Piedmont's propositions, which "tendent à engager le Roy à la guerre avec l'Espagne, (et que) la Seigneurie de Venize contribue comme faict l'Angleterre et Holande" — the Savoyard push Monzón defeated six weeks later. | [`targets/herbault1626/`](targets/herbault1626/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/herbault1626.html) |

Both pairs aligned group by group: a partial table of Philip II's general cipher Cg.13 (some 70 syllables in rows of five, three ending marks, c. 50 code groups), consistent with Tomokiyo's published fragment at every overlap. Cross-reading the letters reads groups the decipherers left blank (pur respeto, mes vuestro, san negocio; bur persona, dom dicho, dur porque, pen mucho from Phelippes's interlinear f. 5), shows tur to be a miscopy of tum, and corrects the copyist's "Francia" for dun (England: the fleet's return, "entre ellos y el de Bearne", "sus armaçones") and "si se hiziesse" for the King's "si yo hiziesse". He marked them undeciphered (no contemporary decipherment, not unsolved); no reading of them was published. The cipher is a simple substitution on 22 letters (a=7, e=11, f=1 … z=22; ff=66, mm=67, nn=68, ss=72); applied to f. 370 it decodes the opening **letter-for-letter, 81 of 81 characters**, against a contemporary clear copy found *independently* of the key — through the old Harlay pièce numbers (224, 226) that Berger de Xivrey cites — in Brienne 13 = BnF NAF 6984 fol. 102v: "le cardinal de Gondy aura passé pres de vous, il y peult ja avoir quelque jours, lequel vous aura fait…". Adds an **omega-shaped sign for *d* not in Tomokiyo's table**. Both letters read (Brienne foll. 102v, 104v); class A, not B. **Open:** only 81 characters of f. 370 transcribed group by group, f. 373 none. | Giovanni Battista Pallotto (Vienna) → Cardinal Francesco Barberini, 28 ciphered despatches, BAV Barb.lat. 6960 (DECODE R286–R313, "Non-decrypted"; catalogue 237, class C) | 4 Aug – 29 Nov 1629 | 20–21 Sept 2026 | complete | **Reading audit, 22–23 Oct 2026** (George Lasry: a reading must build coherent sentences): already solved, read from existing decipherment (Kiewning 1897). 22 of 28 records read (95,069 of 118,931 symbols, 0.799) by aligning the 6956-key decode with Kiewning's print and reading the regest-only passages from the figures; R291, R300, R301, R302, R304 and R313 are decoded, not read. Solved earlier by **Contents in print since 1897; key found by others.** Hans Kiewning, *Nuntiaturberichte aus Deutschland* IV/2, *Nuntiatur des Pallotto 1628–1630*, Bd. 2: *1629* (Berlin 1897), prints these despatches from the Roman decipherments (ASV Nunz. di Germania 119); confirmed here against the register (R286 = pp. 5–6 = Nr. 153; p. 13 = its *Beilage II*). **The key is the key of the sibling volume Barb.lat. 6956**, broken by Norbert Biermann and Thomas Bosbach in March 2018 and filed as documents on DECODE R215. George Lasry had found years ago that 6960 uses it (DECODE was never updated), and told us on 21 Sept 2026. The system is two-digit homophones plus syllable/word groups, three-digit nomenclator groups (200–999), and the digits 1 and 8 as nulls. Applied to DECODE's transcriptions of all 28 records, it reads running Italian: R286 opens *ho riceuto la risposta datami in scritto*, Nr. 153 verbatim. About 1,750 three-digit groups are missing from the 6956 key and stay open. This project's own attacks (crib alignment, homophonic annealing, a fixed-width corpus search over the whole edition) all failed. They did establish, correctly, that the cipher uses deterministic groups of varying length, and that mix of widths is why every fixed-width test missed. Catalogue 236 (6956) was removed as solved by others. | [`targets/pallotto1629/`](targets/pallotto1629/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/pallotto1629.html) |
| Cardinal Francesco Barberini (Rome) → Giovanni Battista Ceva, nuncio extraordinary in Paris, two letters, AAV Segretario di Stato, Francia, doss. 346 nos. 2 and 11 (DECODE R75, R84; catalogue 248, class A) | 25 Sept, 18 Dec 1632 | 20 Sept 2026 | complete | **Reading audit, 22–23 Oct 2026** (George Lasry: a reading must build coherent sentences): read from existing decipherment, complete (was read with known key). R75 and R84 have complete contemporary decipherments among the png attachments of their DECODE records (f. 82r, f. 136r), never opened before; transcribed and aligned with the decode (2,400 of 2,400 tokens), they fix eight values (104 = Olandesi, not Svedesi). R75 opens with Schomberg's victory at Castelnaudary, not the Swedish one. **Two undeciphered letters of the dossier deciphered.** The entry listed five ciphertexts as a transcription job; in fact George Lasry reconstructed the key in 2020 and read four of the eleven letters, covering three of the five, while no reading of R75 and R84 was published. System: two-figure homophones and syllables, every `2x` a null, `6` a word separator occurring inside no code, and three-digit nomenclator elements (a prefix digit plus an ordinary code, not the four digits the DECODE note claims). Beam search over the unseparated stream under `it-cinquecento` interpolated with a model built from the dossier's own clear text; leave-one-out against Lasry's four letters, 95.8% of tokens, nomenclator recall 0.70. Controlled against the 1632 interlinear on R75: 1,105 letters, 77.5% agreement. Content: 25 Sept, the Swedish victories make *l'accomodamento* easier and *l'unione* harder, and the reach of that power *sino al Mediterraneo* is a danger to France; 18 Dec, a month after Lützen, the fear of *conseguenze che tengano disunita per un lungo pezzo la Francia … impotente a soccorrer li suoi alleati*. Six nomenclator elements read (474 mente, 495 quanto, 498 tanto, 830 Francia, 854 guerra, 149 piazza), the first solved in this dossier. | Alonso Sánchez (Venice) → Charles V, and Lope Hurtado de Mendoza, 1522, RAH Salazar 9/23–9/26 (DECODE R9593–R9657, 29 records "non-decrypted"; catalogue Tomokiyo list, class B) | 1522 | 20 Sept 2026 | partial | **Reading audit, 22–23 Oct 2026** (George Lasry: a reading must build coherent sentences): read in part, read from existing decipherment (was read with known key). R9635, R9613 and R9641 read on 23 Oct from contemporary decipherments found on their own DECODE images (ff. 22r, 206r–207v, 187r–188r; R9642 is R9641's duplicate cipher); R9653 and R9593 are decoded, not read; about 0.75 of the words worked (estimated), and 48 Sánchez records stay unread. Solved earlier by **Reconstructed and published by Satoshi Tomokiyo, [*Correspondence in Cipher of Imperial Ambassadors Alonso Sanchez and Juan Manuel (1522)*](https://cryptiana.web.fc2.com/code/AlonsoSanchez.htm), 6 Sept 2025** — alphabet, ~177 code groups and the opening lines of R9593–R9632, from the contemporary decipherment on R9605. Added here: the catalogue entry corrected (the run spans 9/23–9/26 not 9/24; every record is dated **1522**, so the July 1523 treaty is not in it; nine records are **Lope Hurtado de Mendoza**; almost all carry a contemporary decipherment, so they were unedited rather than unread). The nomenclator is alphabetically ordered (C1×V×C3, sorted by C1,C3,V), which fills the numeral run: `cab` = xv, `cef` = xxvi and `cif` = xxvii predicted and then **confirmed in text**, resolving Tomokiyo's uncertain "[xxviii?]" as `cof`. Two letters outside his coverage read in part: **R9653** (20 Nov 1522) — the siege of Rhodes in clear, the foot cipher "cartas de Francia de xxvi y xxvii del passado"; and **R9635** (16 Sept 1522) — a whole page of cipher with a patchy interlinear decipherment, "los de esta republica", "del señor infante", "ducados allende de los otros xviii mil", "mas de xv mil ducados", glossed *fuessen restituydas sus bienes*. Lope Hurtado's groups take finals (`tep`, `tap`, -z) absent from Sánchez's key — a third cipher, unreconstructed. Open: 27 records, the spelled-out letter runs on R9635 f. 20r, the head paragraph of R9653 (ink bleed), Lope Hurtado's key. | [`targets/sanchez1522/`](targets/sanchez1522/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/sanchez1522.html) |

On that control the true key scores far below gibberish keys, so ciphertext-only was closed. No key in Arcinsys, HCPortal or DECODE (112 Marburg keys of 1710–99 listed). About 3,400 hypotheses registered before testing (some 640 sets): an ending slot chosen by the name's last sign; line breaks never split bound pairs or name + ending; numerals fixed with what they count; object kind (not region) shapes the texts; the size of a number decides what is counted. The Tamil Nadu graffiti's Indus parallels are about what chance gives (blind control against Linear A, Linear B, Cypro-Minoan), their signs do not combine the Indus way, and the Indus-like ones are not the oldest. No sound value | [`targets/indus/`](targets/indus/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/indus.html) |

(18 Sept 2026): all 23 lines. The only letter of the Norfolk series with no contemporary decipherment; Tomokiyo marked it undeciphered (no contemporary decipherment, not unsolved), rebuilt the key from ff. 66/69/75 and overlaid a partial letter-by-letter decode. Key checked on f. 66r against its decipherment, then applied to new BL IIIF images stitched from 1,024-px tiles. Mary asks Norfolk to write through the Bishop of Ross or "your sister Scrup" and not in his own hand; Elizabeth "sueves me the veyt of Murray death"; "the brut is that Murton is gon". | Walsingham letter-book, Burghley → Walsingham, Westminster–Paris, BL Harley MS 260 f. 395 (DECODE R8362, catalogued as 'W. Baurleighs? to unknown recipient, 27 Jan 1572'; C) | 24 Jan 1572/3 | 22 Sept 2026 | &mdash; | **Not a cipher: a clear letter, read in full.** Letter-book copy of Burghley's letter, printed in Digges 1655; DECODE's '27' is '24th'. The only signs are boxed 9s after the cover-names Lyons and Roome, which mark them as cover-names. Who they stand for is not known | [`targets/r8362/`](targets/r8362/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/r8362.html) |
| C. Ben. Schaeblin (The Hague) to Mr Jones, BL Add MS 32256 f. 235 (DECODE R9218, catalogued as "Mr. Jones (Hague) to Ben. Schaeblin", nomenclator, numerical, non-decrypted; catalogue 72, class A) | 16 Jan 1753 | 21 Sept 2026 | &mdash; | **Not a cipher: a clear English letter, read in full.** Sender and receiver are swapped on DECODE. Schaeblin proposes a new French cipher of 3000 numbers with blanks, modelled on the English one, and disguises by reversing or rotating the figures of each group or adding a dummy figure, each marked by a "Change the Cypher" group; the only numbers are worked examples on 1753. The French cipher itself not looked for | [`targets/jones1753/`](targets/jones1753/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/jones1753.html) |
| Charles V → Prince Philip, minute on an exchange with Anton Fugger, AGS Estado leg. 1563 fol. 572 (DECODE R9970, catalogued after Galende 1994 as "Andrea del Burgo (Ferrara) to Chancellor Gattinara, 26 Oct 1527", alphabet, graphic signs, numerical, non-decrypted; catalogue 198, class C) | 1543–1554 | 28 Sept 2026 | &mdash; | **Not a cipher: two drafts of a clear Spanish minute "al príncipe".** The Emperor tells Prince Philip of a further exchange of 110,770 ducats ("CX U DCC LXX") with "Antonio Fucar", principal and interest to be paid at term. Philip was born in May 1527, so not 1527. The Roman numerals and U thousands sign account for DECODE's tags. The Burgo letter of 26 Oct 1527 is not on the record and was not found on PARES, in CSP Spain III.2 or in Tomokiyo | [`targets/burgo1527/`](targets/burgo1527/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/burgo1527.html) |
| Count Visconti (Brussels) with Marquis Visconti (London), BL Add MS 32270 f. 42 (DECODE R7931, "Count Visconti to Visconti", "partially decrypted" ciphertext; catalogue 73, class A) | 1727 | 21 Sept 2026 | &mdash; | **Not a letter: the key.** The Deciphering Branch's fair copy of the Italian nomenclator it rebuilt (1–300, 193 filled: letters with homophones, doubled letters, CV syllables, names from 1 Walpole, 22 Sinzendorff, 146 Ostenda, 170 Townshend to 254 Staremberg), endorsed "Count Visconti at Bruxelles with Marq.s Visconti here, 1727, Italian Cypher"; R7932 is its working draft, R7933 a French key for the same pair. Nothing enciphered on the leaf; no letter in this cipher in the volume | [`targets/visconti1727/`](targets/visconti1727/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/visconti1727.html) |
| Mr Eichel → unknown recipient, "Prussia 1758", TNA SP 106/7 image 0020 (DECODE R595, "Non-decrypted", "probably from Russia"; catalogue 65, class A) | 1758 | 21 Sept 2026 | &mdash; | **No message: nulls and blank key cells only.** DECODE's key R594 does not fit. Key R596 (same volume) declares every number with a 7 and 1–503, 1115–1198, 1483–1999, 2600–2802 null: 117/139 groups are nulls, and 21 of the other 22 are cells R596 leaves blank (7% of live numbers). A dummy sheet; R596 undated, its tie to Eichel open | [`targets/eichel1758/`](targets/eichel1758/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/eichel1758.html) |
| Instructions for the cipher with Baron Stosch and Mr Walton, TNA SP 106/7 f. 33 (DECODE R597, "unknown sender to unknown recipient", "partially decrypted", "lines unsolved"; catalogue 64, class B) | c. 1731 | 21 Sept 2026 | &mdash; | **Not a letter: an instruction sheet, read in full.** Two keys (A black, B red) of three-digit syllable groups 100–999, 371 = change of key, groups run on unseparated, nulls 110, 220 &c. and all above 900. The worked example *Les Adherens du Pretendant se donnent beaucoup de mouvement à Rome* has its syllables written over every group; DECODE's unsolved lines are the same example run together. Stosch = "John Walton", Walpole's spy on the Stuart court; Rome corrected to Florence dates it c. 1731. 702 (du/up) and 023 doubtful; keys A/B not on the record | [`targets/sp106r597/`](targets/sp106r597/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/sp106r597.html) |
| W. Loeschner → unknown, Vienna, ÖStA HHStA Staatskanzlei Interiora, Chiffrenschlüssel Kt. 20 ff. 30–40, DECODE R2227 (catalogue, 'W. Loeschnen?') | 1 Jul 1816 | 21 Sept 2026 | &mdash; | **Not a cipher.** A clear German memorandum, *Zusätze und Abänderungen*, proposing changes to the instruction for using the Staatskanzlei's cipher keys, signed W. Loeschner. Its figures are worked examples with the plaintext written above them (le Roi d'Espagne vient de nommer … M. le Duc de San Carlos). Nothing hidden | [`targets/loeschnen1816/`](targets/loeschnen1816/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/loeschnen1816.html) |
| Unknown sender → unknown recipient, BL Sloane MS 3188 ff. 109–169, DECODE R8506–R8530 (catalogue 138 + 139, class C) | 17th c. (leaves dated 1594–95) | 21 Sept 2026 | &mdash; | **Not a cipher.** The 25 leaves (DECODE R8506–R8530) after John Dee's spirit diaries are notes attributed to William Shippen, in 17th-century English stroke shorthand with names, titles and years in longhand: an index of Dee's Actions, a year list of his writings, a note on Kelley, Pucci and Prague (1595). Shorthand not identified or read | [`targets/sloane3188/`](targets/sloane3188/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/sloane3188.html) |
| Frederic, Re di Prussia → unknown recipient, BL Add MS 61575 f. 95, DECODE R8774 (catalogue 98, class A) | 1702–23 (volume span) | 21 Sept 2026 | &mdash; | **Not a cipher.** No numbers on the page: seven lines of plain French, spelled by ear and split into syllables, thanking the recipient for teaching the writer *d'écrire en chiffre* and wishing them a good journey. Writer and recipient unidentified | [`targets/prussia1702/`](targets/prussia1702/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/prussia1702.html) |
| Hyde's ciphered superscriptions | 1659–60 | 15 Sept 2026 | &mdash; | Dummy numbers "only to puzzle the Enemy", per the 1724 editor and the full Hyde–Barwick key of 1721 | [`targets/hyde/`](targets/hyde/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/hyde.html) |
| Chinese gold bar cryptograms, Shanghai | 1933 | 15 Sept 2026 | &mdash; | Almost exactly ten of every letter; flatter than any cipher of a real text can be. No message | [`targets/goldbar/`](targets/goldbar/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/goldbar.html) |
| Roosevelt cryptogram, number block | 1935 | 16 Sept 2026 | &mdash; | A permutation of 1 to 52, each once, padded with zeros; its statistics are those of a list written by hand, and the testable ordered-key cipher readings fail while matched controls succeed. Ernst's 2017 doodle claim confirmed. No message | [`targets/roosevelt/`](targets/roosevelt/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/roosevelt.html) |
| Beale Paper no. 1 | 1885 | 14 Sept 2026 | &mdash; | Fabrication; evidence in notes, book-cipher scan over Gutenberg negative | [`targets/beale/`](targets/beale/) |
| Intercepted League and Spanish letters summarised for the duc de Nevers, Sept–Oct 1589, BnF fr. 3977 no. 96 (catalogue item 31; *"whether the intercepts survive in the volume is unknown"*) | 1589 | 20 Sept 2026 | &mdash; | **They do not survive, and the summary is not in cipher.** No. 96 = f. 318 = canvas 520 is a digest **in clear French**, fifteen numbered points: Mercœur asking Spain for help, powder and money carried to Nantes by Diego Maldonado *"avec le chiffre général d'Espagne"*, Mauléon and *"la lettre escripte du chiffre qu'ilz ont ensemble"*, Mendoza at Bayonne on Arques and Dieppe, Spain pressing the Pope to renew the excommunication. **Every** piece in the volume dated September or October 1589 (nos. 81, 84, 85, 89–97) is in clear, so there is no ciphertext to attack under no. 96. **What the sweep found instead: eleven *other* intercepted ciphered letters in the same volume, Feb–Aug 1589, each with its contemporary decipherment written word by word above the cipher** — nos. 29, 31, 38, 39, **43–45** (three from Mendoza to the duke of Parma, filed in a multi-folio entry a naive regex drops), 47, 48, 49, 71. Eleven complete cipher-and-plaintext pairs for Spanish ciphers of 1589; none transcribed, no key rebuilt | [`targets/nevers1589/`](targets/nevers1589/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/nevers1589.html) |
| Zhongshan telegrams | c.1938 | 15 Sept 2026 | &mdash; | No corpus exists online; the premise of the list entry was wrong | [`targets/zhongshan/`](targets/zhongshan/) |
| Blancmesnil → Nevers (fr. 3633 f. 24) | 1589? | 16 Sept 2026 | &mdash; | No ciphertext online: fr. 3633 is not digitised (the source page's catalogue link goes to fr. 4736); the Potier-Nevers key of 1589 is the candidate once a copy exists; sibling letters with decipherments sit in fr. 3616/3621 (DECODE R9434-9448, images restricted) | [`targets/blancmesnil/`](targets/blancmesnil/) |
| Charles II → Duke of Hamilton | 1650 |  | &mdash; | Copy order for NRS GD406/1/2197 (open) | [`targets/hamilton/`](targets/hamilton/) |

<!-- Beatrice source identification: catalogue 160. -->
Five further list entries turned out to be solved by others with nothing to add here: Perwich 1670 (Brown; Lasry, Biermann and Tomokiyo, 2025), the Milroy telegrams (Bean, 2026), the Feynman ciphers 2 and 3 (2023), the Confederate Navy dictionary code (2026) and the Mazarin–Bordeaux letter of 1654 (Lasry, 2025). They are not counted in the results above; the directories `targets/perwich/`, `targets/milroy/`, `targets/feynman/` and `targets/barney/` hold only the pointer to the published solution.
